### Task 16: Signed release, release-please and repository docs

**Files:**
- Modify: `app/build.gradle.kts`
- Create: `release-please-config.json`, `.release-please-manifest.json`, `.github/workflows/release.yml`
- Create: `README.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `SECURITY.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/bug_report.yml`, `feature_request.yml`, `config.yml`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: the finished app (Tasks 1 to 15); `appVersion` with its `x-release-please-version` marker (Task 1).
- Produces: `nytka-<version>.apk` (the `oss` flavor, release build, signed) attached to each GitHub release; the environment variables `NYTKA_KEYSTORE_FILE`, `NYTKA_KEYSTORE_PASSWORD`, `NYTKA_KEY_ALIAS`, `NYTKA_KEY_PASSWORD` that switch signing on; repository secrets `NYTKA_KEYSTORE_BASE64`, `NYTKA_KEYSTORE_PASSWORD`, `NYTKA_KEY_ALIAS`, `NYTKA_KEY_PASSWORD`.

**Precondition (owner action):** the repository is `nytka-app/android`, and the release keystore exists. Creating the keystore and storing its secrets is the maintainer's, not the executor's: the key signs every future update, and losing it means no installed copy can ever be updated. Ask before Step 3.

R8 stays off in v0.1 (`isMinifyEnabled = false`): Hilt, Room and kotlinx.serialization ship keep rules, but an APK that fails only in its minified form is a poor first release, and the size saved is small. Turn it on in a later version with a test pass over the release build.

- [ ] **Step 1: Sign release builds when a keystore is given**

In `app/build.gradle.kts`, inside `android { }`, add:

```kotlin
    // Release signing comes from the environment (CI decodes the keystore from a secret);
    // without it, release builds stay unsigned and cannot be installed, which is the point.
    val keystore = System.getenv("NYTKA_KEYSTORE_FILE")
    signingConfigs {
        if (keystore != null) {
            create("release") {
                storeFile = file(keystore)
                storePassword = System.getenv("NYTKA_KEYSTORE_PASSWORD")
                keyAlias = System.getenv("NYTKA_KEY_ALIAS")
                keyPassword = System.getenv("NYTKA_KEY_PASSWORD")
            }
        }
    }

    buildTypes {
        release {
            isMinifyEnabled = false
            if (keystore != null) signingConfig = signingConfigs.getByName("release")
        }
    }
```

Check it with a throwaway key (never the real one, never committed; `*.jks` is gitignored):

```bash
keytool -genkeypair -keystore "$TMPDIR/throwaway.jks" -alias test -keyalg RSA -keysize 2048 -validity 1 \
  -storepass throwaway -keypass throwaway -dname "CN=throwaway"
NYTKA_KEYSTORE_FILE="$TMPDIR/throwaway.jks" NYTKA_KEYSTORE_PASSWORD=throwaway NYTKA_KEY_ALIAS=test \
  NYTKA_KEY_PASSWORD=throwaway ./gradlew assembleOssRelease assemblePlayRelease
"$(ls -d "$ANDROID_HOME"/build-tools/* | sort -V | tail -1)/apksigner" verify --print-certs \
  app/build/outputs/apk/oss/release/app-oss-release.apk
```

Expected: both flavors build, and `apksigner` prints `CN=throwaway`.

- [ ] **Step 2: Add release-please and the release workflow**

`release-please-config.json`:

```json
{
  "$schema": "https://raw.githubusercontent.com/googleapis/release-please/main/schemas/config.json",
  "release-type": "simple",
  "include-component-in-tag": false,
  "bump-minor-pre-major": true,
  "packages": {
    ".": {
      "package-name": "nytka-android",
      "extra-files": [
        { "type": "generic", "path": "app/build.gradle.kts" }
      ]
    }
  }
}
```

`.release-please-manifest.json`:

```json
{
  ".": "0.0.0"
}
```

The generic updater rewrites the line marked `// x-release-please-version` (`val appVersion = "0.0.0"`), so `versionName` and the derived `versionCode` follow each release. The first release PR proposes `0.1.0` (tag `v0.1.0`) from the `feat` commits of Tasks 2 to 15.

`.github/workflows/release.yml`:

```yaml
name: release

on:
  push:
    branches: [main]

permissions:
  contents: read

jobs:
  release-please:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    permissions:
      contents: write
      pull-requests: write
    outputs:
      created: ${{ steps.release.outputs.release_created }}
      tag: ${{ steps.release.outputs.tag_name }}
      version: ${{ steps.release.outputs.version }}
    steps:
      - id: release
        uses: googleapis/release-please-action@45996ed1f6d02564a971a2fa1b5860e934307cf7 # v5.0.0
        with:
          config-file: release-please-config.json
          manifest-file: .release-please-manifest.json

  # In the same workflow on purpose: a tag pushed with GITHUB_TOKEN starts no other workflow.
  apk:
    needs: release-please
    if: needs.release-please.outputs.created == 'true'
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    permissions:
      contents: write
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@3d3c42e5aac5ba805825da76410c181273ba90b1 # v7.0.1
        with:
          ref: ${{ needs.release-please.outputs.tag }}
      - uses: actions/setup-java@de7274f081f381c8f8158605e0321c36c376e2e6 # v6.0.1
        with:
          distribution: temurin
          java-version: 21
      - uses: gradle/actions/setup-gradle@3f5f9adaf7d9fecd50b5935e54106014257a94e6 # v6.4.0
      - name: Decode the keystore
        env:
          KEYSTORE: ${{ secrets.NYTKA_KEYSTORE_BASE64 }}
        run: echo "$KEYSTORE" | base64 -d > "$RUNNER_TEMP/nytka.jks"
      - name: Build both flavors, release
        env:
          NYTKA_KEYSTORE_FILE: ${{ runner.temp }}/nytka.jks
          NYTKA_KEYSTORE_PASSWORD: ${{ secrets.NYTKA_KEYSTORE_PASSWORD }}
          NYTKA_KEY_ALIAS: ${{ secrets.NYTKA_KEY_ALIAS }}
          NYTKA_KEY_PASSWORD: ${{ secrets.NYTKA_KEY_PASSWORD }}
        run: ./gradlew check assembleOssRelease assemblePlayRelease
      - name: Verify the signature and attach the APK
        env:
          GH_TOKEN: ${{ github.token }}
          TAG: ${{ needs.release-please.outputs.tag }}
          VERSION: ${{ needs.release-please.outputs.version }}
        run: |
          apksigner="$(ls -d "$ANDROID_HOME"/build-tools/* | sort -V | tail -1)/apksigner"
          "$apksigner" verify app/build/outputs/apk/oss/release/app-oss-release.apk
          cp app/build/outputs/apk/oss/release/app-oss-release.apk "nytka-$VERSION.apk"
          gh release upload "$TAG" "nytka-$VERSION.apk" --repo "$GITHUB_REPOSITORY"
      - name: Remove the keystore
        if: always()
        run: rm -f "$RUNNER_TEMP/nytka.jks"
```

The release PR that release-please opens with `GITHUB_TOKEN` does not trigger `ci.yml`; if branch protection requires CI on it, close and reopen the PR by hand, or give release-please a GitHub App token (owner decision, same as the server).

- [ ] **Step 3: Store the release key (owner action)**

The maintainer, not the executor, runs this once, keeps `nytka-release.jks` and its passwords in a password manager and an offline backup, and deletes the working copy:

```bash
keytool -genkeypair -v -keystore nytka-release.jks -alias nytka -keyalg RSA -keysize 4096 -validity 36500
base64 -i nytka-release.jks | gh secret set NYTKA_KEYSTORE_BASE64 --repo nytka-app/android
gh secret set NYTKA_KEYSTORE_PASSWORD --repo nytka-app/android
gh secret set NYTKA_KEY_ALIAS --repo nytka-app/android --body nytka
gh secret set NYTKA_KEY_PASSWORD --repo nytka-app/android
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Write the repository docs**

The README is the whole of done-when 1: a new user installs the APK and reaches "Recording" with it alone, so every control it names must carry exactly that label in the app (Step 5 checks). The other files mirror the server's.

`README.md`:

````markdown
# Nytka for Android

The phone half of [Nytka](https://github.com/nytka-app/server/blob/main/docs/vision.md), a companion
app and self-hosted server for the Omi AI necklace. The app takes the pendant's audio over
Bluetooth, keeps it in a queue on the phone and uploads it to your own
[Nytka server](https://github.com/nytka-app/server), which transcribes it and serves the
conversations back. Omi's cloud sees none of it.

Status: v0.1, capture and transcription. What v0.1 does and does not do:
[the v0.1 spec](https://github.com/nytka-app/server/blob/main/docs/specs/v0.1.md).

## What you need

- A phone on Android 12 or later.
- An Omi pendant on consumer firmware 3.0.x. Nytka checks the pendant's audio codec when it
  connects and tells you if the firmware needs an update.
- A Nytka server the phone can reach, and its token (`Nytka__AdminToken` in the server's `.env`).
  The [server README](https://github.com/nytka-app/server#run-it) sets one up with Docker Compose.

## Install

1. Uninstall the official Omi app, or at least force-stop it: two apps cannot hold the pendant at
   once. To update the pendant's firmware later, install it again, update, and uninstall it.
2. On the phone, download `nytka-<version>.apk` from
   [Releases](https://github.com/nytka-app/android/releases/latest) and open it. Android asks once
   whether your browser may install apps; allow it. From a computer: `adb install nytka-<version>.apk`.

A newer APK installs over the old one the same way and keeps the queue and the settings.

## First run

Charge the pendant and keep it next to the phone.

1. **Server.** Enter the server's address (`https://…`) and the token, and tap **Test connection**.
   The next step opens once the server answers.
2. **Permissions.** Tap **Allow** and grant *Nearby devices* (and *Notifications*, which Android 13 and
   later ask for). Nytka never uses
   the phone's microphone.
3. **Consent.** Read the note, tick **I understand** and tap **Continue**.
4. **Pairing.** Turn the pendant on, tap **Pair pendant** and pick it in the list that opens.

The chip at the top now reads **Recording**, with a red dot and the pendant's battery. Speech shows
up under **Conversations** a few minutes after it is said.

A server on a VPN such as Tailscale or WireGuard, without HTTPS, needs the **Private network**
switch on the server step. It allows plain `http://`, which carries your audio unencrypted: use it
only on a network you trust.

## Everyday use

- **Mute** with a double tap on the pendant: one long buzz means muted, two short buzzes mean
  recording again. **Mute** in the notification and on the status card does the same. Nothing is
  recorded while muted, and Nytka stays muted when the pendant reconnects.
- **Offline**, audio waits on the phone and uploads once the server answers again, with the times
  it was said. The queue holds up to 1 GiB, about 70 hours. Past 80% Nytka warns you; when it is
  full, the oldest audio goes first.
- **Alerts** come when the pendant has been away for 5 minutes, the server has been unreachable
  for 15 minutes, or the pendant battery reaches 20%.
- **Delete** a conversation from its ⋮ menu: its transcript and audio go from your server.
- **After a reboot**, recording starts once the phone has been unlocked and the pendant is in
  range; Nytka need not be opened.
- The **Device** tab shows the pendant and the server, and holds the settings: server address,
  token and the private-network switch.

## When something is off

| What you see | What to do |
|---|---|
| "Pendant not supported", or a message about the audio codec | Update the pendant's firmware with the official Omi app, then uninstall that app again. |
| The chip stays on "Waiting" | The official Omi app still holds the pendant, Bluetooth is off, or the pendant is out of range or flat. |
| "Paused: The server refused the token." | The server's token changed. Enter the new one under Device → Settings; nothing queued is lost. |
| "Unreachable since …" | The phone cannot reach the server. Audio waits in the queue and uploads by itself. |
| The recording notification disappears on its own | Some phones stop background apps. Set Nytka's battery use to *Unrestricted* in its system settings; [dontkillmyapp.com](https://dontkillmyapp.com) has the steps for each maker. |

## What stays on the phone

Audio waiting to upload, in the app's private storage, until the server has it; the settings; and
the token, encrypted with a key that never leaves the Android Keystore. Android backups and
phone-to-phone transfers skip all of it, so a new phone starts with first run. The app talks to
your server and nothing else: no analytics, no crash reporting, no Omi cloud.

## Developer mode

Tap the version under Device → About seven times. Developer mode shows the Bluetooth packet rate
and loss, the queue, the last upload and the server's status. It has a fake pendant that replays a
bundled text-to-speech recording, a button that saves the next 60 seconds of frames as a test
fixture, and **Copy debug report** for bug reports, which never includes the token. A saved fixture
holds the voices of whoever spoke: keep it private.

## Build from source

JDK 21 and the Android SDK with platform 37.0; see [CONTRIBUTING.md](CONTRIBUTING.md).

```bash
./gradlew assembleOssDebug
adb install app/build/outputs/apk/oss/debug/app-oss-debug.apk
```

`oss` is the flavor Releases publishes; `play` is kept for a later store listing and only has to
compile.

## License

Apache-2.0, see [LICENSE](LICENSE) and [NOTICE](NOTICE).

Nytka is an independent project, not affiliated with or endorsed by Based Hardware; "Omi" is a
trademark of its owner. Nytka records the people around the wearer: you are responsible for
following the recording and privacy laws where you use it.
````

`CLAUDE.md`:

````markdown
# Nytka for Android

Android app for the Omi pendant: reassembles its Opus frames from Bluetooth notifications, queues
them in Room, uploads them as chunks to a Nytka server and shows the conversations the server
transcribes. What v0.1 must do: `docs/specs/v0.1.md` in nytka-app/server; how it was built:
`docs/plans/`.

## Stack

Kotlin 2.4 (AGP 9 built-in Kotlin) · Jetpack Compose, Material 3 · Hilt with KSP · Room · DataStore
with a Keystore-encrypted token · WorkManager · OkHttp, kotlinx.serialization · JUnit 4,
Robolectric, MockWebServer, Turbine · ktlint, detekt.

| Module | Holds |
|---|---|
| `:pendant` | `Pendant`, `FrameAssembler`, `OmiPendant` (Bluetooth), `FakePendant`; no UI |
| `:core` | chunk format, `FrameQueue` (Room), settings, `NytkaApi`, `Uploader`; tested on the JVM |
| `:app` | Hilt wiring, `CaptureController` and the services, alerts, Compose screens |

## Commands

```bash
./gradlew check assembleOssDebug assemblePlayDebug   # what CI runs
./gradlew :core:testDebugUnitTest
./gradlew :app:testOssDebugUnitTest --tests '*CaptureControllerTest'
./gradlew ktlintFormat                               # before every commit
./gradlew installOssDebug && adb logcat | grep -iE "nytka|AndroidRuntime"
```

## Architecture invariants

1. **A frame is safe once it is in Room.** `CaptureController` writes each reassembled frame to
   `FrameQueue.add` as it arrives; sealing, the cap and uploads work on what Room holds.
2. **`CaptureController` alone turns the pendant's audio on or off.** It calls `setAudio(!muted)`
   whenever the connection becomes `Connected` or the muted setting changes, so a reconnect never
   resubscribes while muted.
3. **A capture session is one run of the capture service:** a new UUID, sequence numbers from 0
   without holes, and a capture time that is the phone's clock when a frame's last fragment arrived.
4. **The chunk format is shared with the server.** The golden bytes in `ChunkFormatTest` match the
   server's `ChunkFormatTests`; change both or neither.
5. **HTTPS is enforced in code.** The network security config permits cleartext because Android
   cannot allow it per server; `ServerUrl` refuses `http://` unless the private-network switch is on.
6. **Nothing sensitive in logs, errors or the debug report:** no audio, no transcript text, no
   token. `Settings.toString` masks the token, and OkHttp has no logging interceptor.
7. **Never `RECORD_AUDIO`.** The app hears only the pendant.
8. **Real voices never enter this repository.** The fake pendant replays text-to-speech;
   developer-mode fixtures stay on the phone or go to the private research repository.

## Conventions

- AGP's built-in Kotlin: never apply `org.jetbrains.kotlin.android`; KSP, never kapt.
- Hand-written fakes, no mocking library (`FakePendant`, `FakeSettings`, `FakeDeviceActions`). View
  models take interfaces (`SettingsSource`, `InfoClient`, `ConversationsClient`, `DeviceActions`),
  so their tests need no Android.
- Room schemas under `core/schemas/` are committed. A schema change bumps the database version and
  adds a migration, never a destructive fallback: the queue holds audio nobody can record again.
- Pendant facts (UUIDs, packet header, button codes) come from `BasedHardware/omi` at a pinned
  commit; name the file when adding one.
- Conventional commits; release-please turns them into releases and bumps `appVersion` in
  `app/build.gradle.kts`. Every commit uses a GitHub noreply address; the `.githooks` hooks and
  `.private-terms` stay on.
````

`CONTRIBUTING.md`:

````markdown
# Contributing

Issues and pull requests are welcome. For anything larger than a fix, open an issue first so we
can agree on the approach; the [vision](https://github.com/nytka-app/server/blob/main/docs/vision.md)
says what Nytka is and is not. Server problems belong in
[nytka-app/server](https://github.com/nytka-app/server/issues).

## Set up

You need JDK 21, the Android SDK with platform 37.0 and build-tools 37.0.0, and
[gitleaks](https://github.com/gitleaks/gitleaks).

```bash
git config core.hooksPath .githooks
cp .private-terms.example .private-terms   # list what must never appear in this repository
./gradlew check
```

The hooks refuse commits whose author or committer is not a GitHub noreply address, scan staged
files and messages for the terms in `.private-terms`, and run gitleaks. Without a pendant, turn on
developer mode and its fake pendant, which replays `app/src/main/assets/fake_pendant.nytk`.

## Rules

- Conventional commits (`feat:`, `fix:`, `docs:` ...); they drive the changelog and the version.
- Run `./gradlew ktlintFormat` before committing; `check` fails on what it cannot fix.
- Every change comes with tests. Capture tests drive `FakePendant`: never commit a recording of a
  real person, not even your own.
- The chunk format is shared with the server: change its golden bytes in both repositories or in
  neither.
- A Room schema change needs a migration and the exported schema under `core/schemas/`.
- Never log audio, transcript text or tokens, and never put them in the debug report.
- Never request `RECORD_AUDIO`.
````

`SECURITY.md`:

```markdown
# Security policy

## Reporting a vulnerability

Use GitHub private vulnerability reporting on this repository (Security tab → "Report a
vulnerability"). Don't open a public issue. Expect an acknowledgement within 7 days.

## Scope notes

The app holds the token of its user's Nytka server (encrypted with an Android Keystore key) and
audio waiting to upload, and it shows transcripts of the wearer and of the people around them.
Report any way another app can read the queue, the settings or the token, or make Nytka start or
stop capture; any path where audio, a transcript or the token leaks into logs, the debug report,
backups or files; any way audio leaves over plain HTTP while the private-network switch is off;
and any way the pendant keeps recording while the app shows it as muted.

## Supported versions

The latest release.
```

`.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/config.yml`:

```yaml
blank_issues_enabled: false
contact_links:
  - name: Report a vulnerability
    url: https://github.com/nytka-app/android/security/advisories/new
    about: Privately, never in a public issue.
  - name: A server problem
    url: https://github.com/nytka-app/server/issues/new/choose
    about: Transcription, conversations or the server itself.
```

`.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/bug_report.yml`:

```yaml
name: Bug report
description: Something the app does wrong.
labels: [bug]
body:
  - type: input
    id: version
    attributes:
      label: App version
      description: Device tab → About.
    validations:
      required: true
  - type: input
    id: phone
    attributes:
      label: Phone and Android version
      description: e.g. Pixel 8, Android 16.
    validations:
      required: true
  - type: input
    id: pendant
    attributes:
      label: Pendant firmware
      description: Device tab → Pendant, while it is connected.
  - type: textarea
    id: what
    attributes:
      label: What happened, and what you expected
    validations:
      required: true
  - type: textarea
    id: report
    attributes:
      label: Debug report
      description: Developer mode (seven taps on the version) → Copy debug report. It holds no token; read it before posting anyway.
      render: text
```

`.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/feature_request.yml`:

```yaml
name: Feature request
description: Something Nytka should do.
labels: [enhancement]
body:
  - type: textarea
    id: problem
    attributes:
      label: The problem
      description: What you are trying to do, and what gets in the way.
    validations:
      required: true
  - type: textarea
    id: idea
    attributes:
      label: What you would like
```

`NOTICE` and `LICENSE` stay as Task 1 left them. Copy this plan to `docs/plans/2026-09-29-v0.1-android.md`, so the pointer in `CLAUDE.md` resolves.

- [ ] **Step 5: Run everything once more**

```bash
./gradlew clean check assembleOssDebug assemblePlayDebug assembleOssRelease assemblePlayRelease
# Whole labels match with both quotes; those that start longer texts match as prefixes.
for label in '"Test connection"' '"Allow"' '"I understand"' '"Continue"' '"Pair pendant"' '"Recording"' \
  '"Waiting"' '"Mute"' '"Copy debug report"' '"Pendant not supported"' '"Paused: ' '"Unreachable since ' \
  '"Private network '; do
  grep -rqF -- "$label" app/src/main/kotlin || echo "The README names something the app does not show: $label"
done
```

Expected: PASS, with `:core` 35, `:pendant` 17 and `:app` 54 tests in every variant; the release builds come out unsigned without the `NYTKA_*` variables; the loop prints nothing.

- [ ] **Step 6: Commit**

```bash
git add app/build.gradle.kts release-please-config.json .release-please-manifest.json .github \
  README.md CONTRIBUTING.md SECURITY.md CLAUDE.md docs
git commit -m "build: sign release APKs through release-please, and document the app"
```

The release itself, `v0.1.0`, is part of the acceptance task: it comes after the checks on a phone.

---

### Task 17: Acceptance

**Files:**
- Modify: `README.md` (the signing certificate, Step 9)
- Modify in `nytka-app/server`: `docs/specs/v0.1.md` (Step 10); `src/Nytka.Audio/Vad/SpeechDetector.cs` or `src/Nytka.Audio/Batching/BatchRules.cs` only if Step 8 changes a value
- Create in `nytka-app/research` (private): `captures/<date>/*.nytk`, `captures/<date>/NOTES.md`, `notes/acceptance-v0.1.md`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: the finished app (Tasks 1 to 16); the finished server (server plan, Tasks 1 to 15), including `Nytka.Replay --chunks`; developer mode's loss figure, fixture button and debug report (Task 15); both release workflows.
- Produces: `v0.1.0` in both repositories; the spec's open questions 1 to 3 settled with measurements; a dated record of every check in the research repository.

This task is manual: the spec's seven "done when" checks on real hardware, plus the three open questions the checks produce evidence for. The maintainer wears the pendant and talks. An agent executor prepares the commands, runs the server-side queries, writes the results down, and asks before every owner action (the releases, the keystore, anything pushed to a public repository). Checks 2 to 6 run on a debug build of `main`, because `run-as` works only there; check 7 releases; check 1 runs last, on the released APK, because it is about a new user installing it. A failed check means a fix in the task that owns the code, then the check again.

- [ ] **Step 1: Prepare**

Deploy the server from its `main` with the Compose stack of the server plan's Task 15 and a real transcription endpoint, at an address the phone reaches. Keep checkouts of `nytka-app/server` and `nytka-app/research` next to this repository (`../server`, `../research`). On the machine that runs the server's Compose stack, in the directory holding its `docker-compose.yml` and `.env`:

```bash
sql() { docker compose exec -T postgres psql -U nytka -d nytka -Atc "$1"; }
export NYTKA_URL=https://nytka.example.com   # the address the phone uses
export NYTKA_TOKEN=$(grep ^Nytka__AdminToken .env | cut -d= -f2)
api() { curl -fsS -H "Authorization: Bearer $NYTKA_TOKEN" "$NYTKA_URL/api/v1/$1"; }
api info
```

Expected: `{"serverVersion":"…","apiVersion":1}`.

On the phone: uninstall the official Omi app, run `./gradlew installOssDebug` from `main`, go through first run (untimed) and turn developer mode on. Start `../research/notes/acceptance-v0.1.md`:

```markdown
# Nytka v0.1 acceptance

App `<commit>`, server `<commit>`, phone `<model>` on Android `<version>`, pendant firmware `<version>`.

| Check | Date | Result | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1. First run from the README | | | |
| 2. Eight-hour day | | | |
| 3. Thirty minutes offline | | | |
| 4. Double-tap mute | | | |
| 5. Delete | | | |
| 6. Kill, reboot, restart | | | |
| 7. Release | | | |
| Token after a restart | | | |
| Open questions 1, 2, 3 | | | |
```

Every step below ends by filling its row: the date, pass or fail, and the numbers or output that show it.

- [ ] **Step 2: The token survives a restart**

```bash
adb shell am force-stop io.github.nytka_app
adb shell monkey -p io.github.nytka_app 1 > /dev/null   # opens Nytka
```

Expected: the tabs open, not first run; the Device tab's server card reads "Connected to Nytka server …" and the token field "Token (leave empty to keep)"; the chip returns to "Recording". With `NYTKA_TOKEN` set as in Step 1, the stored settings hold no readable token:

```bash
adb shell run-as io.github.nytka_app wc -c files/datastore/settings.preferences_pb
adb shell run-as io.github.nytka_app cat files/datastore/settings.preferences_pb | grep -ac "${NYTKA_TOKEN:0:16}"
```

Expected: a size above 0 (the file exists and is readable), then `0`.

- [ ] **Step 3: Double-tap mute (done-when 4)**

1. With the chip on "Recording", double-tap the pendant. Expected: one long buzz; the chip and the notification read "Muted"; in developer mode "Frames this session" stops growing.
2. While muted, say: "Pineapple harbour, this must never be transcribed."
3. Turn the phone's Bluetooth off, wait ten seconds, turn it on. Expected: the Device tab shows the pendant connected again, with no buzz; still "Muted", and the frame count still stands.
4. Double-tap. Expected: two short buzzes; "Recording". Say: "Lighthouse keeper, this one counts." Stay quiet for 30 seconds.
5. Tap Mute in the notification, then Unmute. Expected: the same buzzes as the double taps.
6. Three minutes later:

```bash
sql "select count(*) from segments where text ilike '%pineapple%'"
sql "select count(*) from segments where text ilike '%lighthouse%'"
```

Expected: `0`, then `1` or more.

- [ ] **Step 4: Delete (done-when 5)**

```bash
id=$(api "conversations?limit=1" | python3 -c 'import json,sys; print(json.load(sys.stdin)["items"][0]["id"])')
for t in segments transcription_batches speech_audio; do sql "select '$t', count(*) from $t where conversation_id = '$id'"; done
```

Expected: every count above 0 (with `RetentionDays` 0, `speech_audio` is already empty; the check then covers the other two). Open that conversation in the app (the newest), ⋮ → Delete → Delete. Expected: the app returns to the list, and the conversation is gone from it. Then:

```bash
for t in conversations segments transcription_batches speech_audio; do
  sql "select '$t', count(*) from $t where $( [ $t = conversations ] && echo id || echo conversation_id ) = '$id'"
done
curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}\n' -H "Authorization: Bearer $NYTKA_TOKEN" "$NYTKA_URL/api/v1/conversations/$id"
```

Expected: four counts of `0`, then `404`.

- [ ] **Step 5: Kill, reboot, restart (done-when 6)**

For each of the three disruptions: count aloud from one, about a number a second, starting a minute before it and going on until two minutes after the chip reads "Recording" again. Write down the clock time of the disruption.

1. **Kill the app** the way the system does (a debug build allows `run-as`):

   ```bash
   date +%T; adb shell run-as io.github.nytka_app kill -9 "$(adb shell pidof io.github.nytka_app)"
   ```

   Expected: the recording notification disappears and comes back within a minute by itself (the service is sticky). Swiping Nytka out of the recent apps changes nothing at all.
2. **Reboot the phone** with `adb reboot`. Unlock it once (Android keeps app data locked until then) and do not open Nytka. Expected: the recording notification returns by itself while the pendant is in range.
3. **Restart the server** on its host: `date +%T; docker compose restart server`. Expected: the app shows nothing but perhaps "Unreachable since …" for a moment.

Three minutes after each, read the conversation around that time in the app. Expected: after the server restart, no number is missing. After the kill and the reboot, every number said before the disruption is there (the one being said at that instant may be cut), and the missing numbers fall only in the stretch while the notification was gone. Then check that every chunk of every capture session reached the server:

```bash
sql "select session_id, min(first_seq), sum(frame_count), max(first_seq + frame_count) from audio_chunks
     group by session_id having min(first_seq) <> 0 or sum(frame_count) <> max(first_seq + frame_count)"
```

Expected: no rows. (A row is a session with a hole: a chunk the app queued that the server never got. The server keeps chunk rows for 7 days, so run this within the week.)

- [ ] **Step 6: Thirty minutes offline (done-when 3, open question 2)**

1. In developer mode, write down "Queue: … MB" and the time.
2. Turn on airplane mode. If Bluetooth went off with it, turn Bluetooth back on; Wi-Fi and mobile data stay off. Expected: within a minute the chip reads "Recording" again.
3. Talk for 30 minutes: a conversation, or reading aloud. Write down when the talk starts and ends. Expected: queued chunks grow by about two a minute; the status card reads "Unreachable since …"; after 15 minutes the "server has been unreachable" alert arrives.
4. Write down "Queue: … MB" again. Megabytes per hour of wear = (end − start) × 2.
5. Turn airplane mode off. Expected: queued chunks fall to 0 within a few minutes, and the talk appears as conversations whose times match the start and end you wrote down, within a minute:

```bash
api "conversations?limit=5" | python3 -m json.tool | grep -E '"(startedAt|endedAt)"'
```

- [ ] **Step 7: An eight-hour day (done-when 2, open questions 1 and 3)**

Wear the pendant for eight hours or more, the phone nearby, without restarting anything. During the day:

1. Keep a short log in `../research/notes/acceptance-v0.1.md`: the start time of each conversation you have, and roughly what it was.
2. Three times, say "Nytka check" and the clock time, then stay quiet for 30 seconds. Expected: each shows up in the list (pull to refresh) within three minutes of being said. Write down the delays.
3. Save five fixtures with "Save the next 60 seconds of frames", only with the consent of everyone audible: (a) you alone in a quiet room, reading a paragraph from a book; (b) the same paragraph on a street or in a café; (c) a conversation with one other person; (d) people speaking across a room, such as a meeting; (e) a minute without talking, with background noise such as a kitchen or music. For (a) and (b), note the book and the paragraph.

At the end of the day:

- Developer mode shows "Lost: …%" under 1%. Copy the debug report into the results file (it holds no token).
- Every conversation in your log is in the list, with a transcript.
- On the server:

```bash
sql "select status, count(*) from transcription_batches where created_at > now() - interval '12 hours' group by status"
api status
```

Expected: only `done` (a `pending` batch may remain from the last minutes); `pendingChunks` 0 once the phone has uploaded. Run the hole query from Step 5 again, count the sessions with `sql "select count(distinct session_id) from audio_chunks"`, and write down both numbers (open question 3).

Pull the fixtures into the research repository and describe them:

```bash
day=$(date +%F)
mkdir -p ../research/captures/$day
adb pull /sdcard/Android/data/io.github.nytka_app/files/fixtures/. ../research/captures/$day/
```

`../research/captures/<day>/NOTES.md` lists each file with its situation (a to e), where it was, who spoke, and for (a) and (b) the book and paragraph.

- [ ] **Step 8: Settle open questions 1 and 3 on a scratch server**

Replay every fixture into a scratch server with an empty database, never into the server the phone uses: that one already holds these frames, split at the queue's 30-second seals, and answers `409` to a chunk that overlaps them differently.

```bash
cd ../server
cat > "$TMPDIR/scratch.env" <<EOF
POSTGRES_PASSWORD=scratch
Nytka__AdminToken=$(openssl rand -hex 24)
NYTKA_PORT=8081
EOF
# append the Nytka__Stt__* lines of the real server's .env to $TMPDIR/scratch.env
scratch() { docker compose -p nytka-scratch --env-file "$TMPDIR/scratch.env" "$@"; }
scratch_sql() { scratch exec -T postgres psql -U nytka -d nytka -Atc "$1"; }
scratch up -d --build --wait
scratch_token=$(grep ^Nytka__AdminToken "$TMPDIR/scratch.env" | cut -d= -f2)   # NYTKA_TOKEN keeps the real server's
date +%T
for f in ../research/captures/<day>/*.nytk; do
  NYTKA_TOKEN=$scratch_token dotnet run --project src/Nytka.Replay -- --server http://127.0.0.1:8081 --chunks "$f"
done
```

Expected: `Frames …: 202` for every chunk. A fixture starts in the middle of its capture session, so the server first waits 10 minutes for the frames before it (open question 3 at work): the transcripts appear 11 to 13 minutes after the replay. Write down that delay and check it against the log:

```bash
scratch logs server | grep -c "waiting for a gap: True"
scratch_sql "select to_char(started_at, 'HH24:MI:SS'), round(extract(epoch from ended_at - started_at)), status
             from transcription_batches order by started_at"
scratch_sql "select c.started_at, string_agg(s.text, ' ' order by s.started_at) from conversations c
             join segments s on s.conversation_id = c.id group by c.id order by c.started_at"
```

For each fixture, compare the transcript with what was said: for (a) and (b), word by word against the paragraph. Count clipped words (missing at the start or end of a stretch of speech) and invented ones (text for a stretch without speech, such as "Thank you." in fixture (e)). Keep the defaults (`SpeechDetector`: `Threshold` 0.5, `NegativeThreshold` 0.35, `MinSilenceMs` 500, `PadMs` 200; `BatchRules`: 1 s pause, 5 s minimum, 30 s maximum) unless the fixtures show a problem. Clipped words mean raising `PadMs` (to 300) or lowering `NegativeThreshold`; invented text means raising `Threshold` (to 0.6). A change is its own `fix(audio):` pull request in the server repository, with its tests updated; then `scratch down -v`, `scratch up -d --build --wait` on that branch, and replay everything again. When done: `scratch down -v` and `rm "$TMPDIR/scratch.env"`.

Open question 3 has its evidence now: the holes counted in Step 7 (how often a chunk really goes missing) and the delay measured here (what a missing chunk costs the transcripts after it). The app uploads in order, so a hole only comes from a chunk the server refused or the cap dropped; unless holes showed up, keep 10 minutes.

- [ ] **Step 9: Release both repositories (done-when 7, owner action)**

The owner merges the open pull requests, then the release pull requests release-please opens (`chore(main): release 0.1.0`) in `nytka-app/server` and in this repository. A release pull request opened with `GITHUB_TOKEN` runs no CI: close and reopen it by hand before merging. Merging creates `v0.1.0` and runs the image and APK jobs. Then:

```bash
gh auth token | docker login ghcr.io -u "$(gh api user --jq .login)" --password-stdin   # the package is private until the owner opens it
docker buildx imagetools inspect ghcr.io/nytka-app/server:0.1.0 | grep -E "Platform:[[:space:]]+linux/(amd64|arm64)"
gh release download v0.1.0 --repo nytka-app/android --pattern 'nytka-*.apk' --dir "$TMPDIR/release"
bt="$(ls -d "$ANDROID_HOME"/build-tools/* | sort -V | tail -1)"
"$bt/apksigner" verify --print-certs "$TMPDIR/release/nytka-0.1.0.apk" | grep "SHA-256"
"$bt/aapt2" dump badging "$TMPDIR/release/nytka-0.1.0.apk" | grep -o "versionCode='[0-9]*' versionName='[^']*'"
for repo in server android; do
  sha=$(gh api repos/nytka-app/$repo/commits/v0.1.0 --jq .sha)
  gh run list --repo nytka-app/$repo --commit "$sha" --json workflowName,conclusion --jq '.[] | "\(.workflowName) \(.conclusion)"'
done
```

Expected: both platforms; one SHA-256 certificate digest, the release key's; `versionCode='100' versionName='0.1.0'`; every run on the tagged commit, CI and release in both repositories, ends in `success`. GHCR creates the package as private: the owner makes `server` public in the organisation's package settings. Deploy it with `NYTKA_VERSION=0.1.0` in the server's `.env`, `docker compose pull && docker compose up -d`; `api info` reads `"serverVersion":"0.1.0"`.

Publish the digest, so anyone can check a download. Under `## Install` in `README.md`, after the paragraph about newer APKs, add (with the digest `apksigner` printed):

```markdown
To check a download, run `apksigner verify --print-certs nytka-<version>.apk` (it comes with the
Android SDK's build-tools). It must print this certificate SHA-256 digest:
`<digest>`. Android itself refuses an update signed with another key.
```

```bash
git switch -c docs/signing-certificate
git add README.md
git commit -m "docs: publish the release signing certificate"
```

and open the pull request.

- [ ] **Step 10: First run from the README (done-when 1)**

Mute, wait until the status card reads "Queued chunks: 0", then remove the debug build: its key differs from the release key, and uninstalling deletes its queue.

```bash
adb uninstall io.github.nytka_app
```

Hand someone who has never set up Nytka the phone, the charged pendant, the server address and token on a note, and the README's address; nothing else. Start a timer when they open the README and stop it when the chip reads "Recording". Expected: under five minutes. Write down the time and every place they hesitated; each hesitation that the README could have prevented becomes a fix in a `docs:` pull request. Over five minutes: fix what slowed them down and repeat with someone else.

- [ ] **Step 11: Record the results**

In `../server/docs/specs/v0.1.md`, turn the status line into `**Status: done.** v0.1.0 released on <date>.` and replace open questions 1 to 3 with what was measured, in the form the spec already uses for settled questions:

```markdown
1. VAD parameters and batch thresholds. Settled on <date> from <n> recordings (<m> minutes: quiet
   room, street, conversation, meeting, background noise): <kept the defaults | changed …>, because
   <the clipped and invented words counted in each>.
2. The queue-full policy. Settled on <date>: drop the oldest chunk. A day of wear queued <x> MB per
   hour, so the 1 GiB cap holds <1024/x> hours offline, and the 80% alert comes <0.2 × 1024/x> hours
   before the first chunk is dropped.
3. The 10-minute wait for a missing chunk. Settled on <date>: <keep | change to …>. An <h>-hour day
   left <k> holes in <s> capture sessions; a replay starting mid-session waited <t> minutes before
   its transcript, which is what one hole costs.
```

```bash
cd ../server && git switch -c docs/settle-open-questions
git add docs/specs/v0.1.md && git commit -m "docs(spec): settle open questions 1 to 3 with acceptance measurements"
cd ../research && git add captures notes
git commit -m "docs: v0.1 acceptance results and captures" && git push
```

Open the server pull request. Nytka v0.1 is done when every row of `notes/acceptance-v0.1.md` reads pass.
